### Agriculture



[Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple and predominant crop.

Agriculture is the primary occupation in South India with nearly 5.31 crore people engaged in agriculture and allied activities in 2021.[^cite_note-269] [Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple food and major crop in the region.[^cite_note-270] South India is a major producer of [spices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spices) with [black pepper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_pepper), [cardamom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardamom), [clove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clove) and [nutmeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nutmeg) grown exclusively in the region.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] Some of the main crops cultivated in South India include [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane), [chilli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile_pepper), [banana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana), [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton), [turmeric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turmeric), [millets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millet) and pulses.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] Other plantation crops include [cashew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cashew), [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee), [tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea), [rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubber), [betel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betel), [areca nut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Areca_nut), [coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut), [bamboo](39029.md) and [cocoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cocoa_bean).[^cite_note-275] The region accounts for 92% of the [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_production_in_India)[^cite_note-Com-259][^cite_note-276] and 85% of the [natural rubber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_rubber) production in the country.[^cite_note-277]

Other major agricultural products include [poultry](23197.md) and [silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk).[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] Being a peninsular region, [aquaculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquaculture) is a major contributor to the economy. As of 2017–18, the region produced 53.68 lakh tonnes fish contributing to nearly 43% of total fish production in India.[^cite_note-280] Like most of the Indian subcontinent, agriculture in the region is largely dependent on seasonal monsoons[^cite_note-281] and monsoon failure often leads to droughts forcing farmers into debt, selling livestock and sometimes into committing suicide.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283]

### Information technology



Bangalore is an [alpha-city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city) and a major information technology hub. Picture: [Infosys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infosys)

South India's urban centres are significant contributors to the Indian and global IT economy. [Bengaluru](44275267.md), [Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai), [Hyderabad](37534.md), [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore), [Kochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kochi) and [Thiruvananthapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiruvananthapuram) are amongst the major information technology (IT) hubs of India.[^cite_note-cnn-Dec2012-284][^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] The presence of these hubs has spurred economic growth and attracted foreign investments and job seekers from other parts of the country.[^cite_note-289] Software exports from South India grossed over ₹640 billion (US\$6.6 billion) in fiscal 2005–06.[^cite_note-290]

### Manufacturing

Manufacturing is various states are governed by state owned industrial corporations like [APIIC (Andhra Pradesh)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_Industrial_Infrastructure_Corporation), KIDC (Karnataka), [KSIDC (Kerala)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_State_Industrial_Development_Corporation), [TIDC (Tamil Nadu)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Nadu_Industrial_Development_Corporation) apart from [central government](553883.md) owned companies. The [automotive industry in Chennai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Chennai) accounts for about 35% of India's overall automotive components and automobile output with [Andhra Pradesh](2377.md) is emerging as another automobile manufacturing hub.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] Coimbatore supplies two-thirds of India's requirements of motors and pumps, and is one of the largest exporters of [wet grinders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet_grinder) and [auto components](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile), as well as jewellery.[^cite_note-293] Another major industry is textiles[^cite_note-294] with the region being home to nearly 60% of the fiber textile mills in India.[^cite_note-295] State owned companies include [Bharat Electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharat_Electronics) (electrical components), [Bharat Heavy Electricals Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharat_Heavy_Electricals_Limited) (power equipments) and [HMT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMT_Limited) (machine tools).[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] [Integral Coach Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integral_Coach_Factory) in Chennai, operated by [Indian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Railways) is the oldest and largest producer of [railway coaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_coach) and [wagons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_wagon).[^cite_note-298]



[ISRO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISRO) rocket launch station at [Sriharikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sriharikota) near Chennai

### Space and Defense

Defense establishments include [Hindustan Aeronautics Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Aeronautics_Limited) in Bangalore which manufactures [fighter aircraft](10929.md), [helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helicopters) and aircraft components.[^cite_note-HAL-Our_History-299] [DRDO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Research_and_Development_Organisation) which is India's premier defense agency operates various facilities in Chennai, Bangalore, Hyderabad and Mysuru in South India.[^cite_note-\:0-300]



[Hogenakkal Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hogenakkal_Falls) on the [Kaveri river](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaveri_river); [Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_India) contributes significantly to the GDP of the region

There are three Ordnance factories in Aruvankadu (Tamil Nadu), [Tiruchirappalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordnance_Factory_Tiruchirappalli) and [Medak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordnance_Factory_Medak).[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302] [AVANI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_Vehicles_Nigam_Limited), headquartered in Chennai manufactures [Armoured fighting vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armoured_fighting_vehicle), [Main battle tanks](26595097.md), tank engines and armored clothing for the use of the [Indian Armed Forces](7171338.md).[^cite_note-mint2021-303][^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] [Bharat Dynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharat_Dynamics_Limited) manufacturers of [ammunitions](18940621.md) and [missile](19594.md) systems and is based in Hyderabad.[^cite_note-bdl-about-306]

[ISRO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISRO) is the premier Indian space agency primarily responsible for performing tasks related to space-based operations, [space exploration](28431.md), international space cooperation and the development of related technologies. It is headquartered in Bangalore.[^cite_note-307] It operates research facilities in [Thiruvananthapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikram_Sarabhai_Space_Center) and [Tirupati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Atmospheric_Research_Laboratory), test facility at [Mahendragiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISRO_Propulsion_Complex), satellite development facilities at [Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U_R_Rao_Satellite_Centre), launch facilities at [Sriharikota](2496664.md) and [Thiruvananthapuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thumba_Equatorial_Rocket_Launching_Station), tracking facilities at [Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Deep_Space_Network), [Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Remote_Sensing_Centre) and [Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_Control_Facility) and [Indian Institute of Space Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Space_Science_and_Technology) at Thiruvananthapuram.[^cite_note-308]

### Tourism

[Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_India) contributes significantly to the GDP of the region, with four states – Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka, and Telangana – among the top 10 states for tourist arrivals, accounting for more than 50% of domestic tourist visits.[^cite_note-309]

Tamil Nadu has the largest tourist inflow in India both domestic and international as of 2020.[^cite_note-310] In 2023, Kerala was listed at the 13th spot in [The New York Times](30680.md)' annual list of places to visit and was the only tourist destination listed from India.[^cite_note-311] Kerala was named by [TIME magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\)) in 2022 among the 50 extraordinary destinations to explore in its list of the World's Greatest Places.[^cite_note-312]

## Culture and heritage

Main article: [South Indian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Indian_culture)

Majority of the region falls under the purview of [South zone cultural center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_Zones_of_India) headquartered at [Thanjavur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur), as per the classification of the [ministry of culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_\(India\)) of the Indian government, for the promotion and preservation of [cultural heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_heritage).[^cite_note-313]

### Clothing



A wedding in traditional South Indian wear



[Kanchipuram silk saris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram_silk_sari) worn by women on special occasions

South Indian women traditionally wear a *[sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari)*, a garment that consists of a drape varying from 15–27 ft (4.6–8.2 m) in length and 2–4 ft (0.61–1.22 m) in breadth, and is typically wrapped around the waist, with one end draped over the shoulder, baring the midriff, as the navel is considered as the source of life and creativity in [Indian philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_philosophy).[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] Ancient Tamil poetry such as the *[Silappadhikaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silappadhikaram)*, describes women in exquisite drapery or sari.[^cite_note-316] [Madisar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madisar) is a typical style worn by Brahmin women from Tamil Nadu.[^cite_note-317] Women wear colourful [silk sarees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_saree) on special occasions such as marriages.[^cite_note-318] [Kanchipuram silk sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram_silk_sari) is a type of [silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk) sari made in the [Kanchipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanchipuram) region in Tamil Nadu, worn by brides during marriages and as dresses for special occasions by most women in South India. It has been recognized as a [Geographical indication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_indication) by the [Government of India](553883.md) in 2005–2006.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320] [Kovai Cora Cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kovai_Cora_Cotton) is a type of [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton) sari made in the Coimbatore.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322]

The men wear a *[dhoti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhoti)*, a 4.5 m (15 ft) long, white rectangular piece of non-stitched cloth often with brightly coloured stripes at the border. It is usually wrapped around the waist and the legs and knotted at the waist.[^cite_note-323] A colourful *[lungi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lungi)* with typical [batik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batik) patterns is the most common form of male attire in the countryside.[^cite_note-Cloth-324]

People in urban areas generally wear tailored clothing, and western dress is popular. Western-style school uniforms are worn by both boys and girls in schools, even in rural areas.[^cite_note-Cloth-324] [Calico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calico), a [plain-woven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plain_weave) textile made from unbleached and often not fully processed cotton, originated at [Kozhikode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kozhikode) in the 11th century CE,[^cite_note-325] and was known as *Chaliyan*.[^cite_note-326] The raw fabric was dyed and printed in bright hues, and [calico prints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chintz) later became popular in the Europe.[^cite_note-327]

### Cuisine

Main article: [South Indian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Indian_cuisine)



A traditional meal served on a banana leaf



[Dosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dosa_\(food\)) made from a fermented batter

[Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple food in South Indian meals.[^cite_note-328] [Coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut) and spices are used extensively in South Indian cuisine. The region has a rich cuisine involving both traditional non-vegetarian and vegetarian dishes comprising rice, [legumes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legume), and [lentils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lentil). Its distinct aroma and flavour is achieved by the blending of [flavourings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flavouring) and spices, including [curry leaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry_tree), [mustard seeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustard_seed), [coriander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coriander), [ginger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginger), [garlic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garlic), [chili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_powder), [pepper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_pepper), [cinnamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinnamon), [cloves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clove), [green cardamom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_cardamom), [cumin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumin), [nutmeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nutmeg), coconut, and [rosewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosewater).[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]

The traditional way of eating a meal involves being seated on the floor, having the food served on a [banana leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_leaf),[^cite_note-331] and using clean fingers of the right hand to take the food into the mouth.[^cite_note-332] After the meal, the fingers are washed; the easily degradable banana leaf is discarded or becomes fodder for cattle.[^cite_note-333] Eating on banana leaves is a custom thousands of years old, imparts a unique flavor to the food, and is considered healthy.[^cite_note-334]

[Idli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idli), [dosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dosa_\(food\)), [uthappam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uthappam), [pesarattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pesarattu), [appam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appam), [pongal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pongal_\(dish\)), and [paniyaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paniyaram) are popular breakfast dishes.[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336] Rice is generally served with [sambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambar_\(dish\)), and [rasam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasam_\(dish\)) for lunch. [Tamil cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_cuisine) includes a typical *virundhu* for lunch with [poriyal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poriyal), [kootu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kootu), and [kuzhambu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuzhambu).[^cite_note-337] Particular dishes include [sevai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sevai), paniyaram, [parotta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parotta), and [opputtu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puran_poli).[^cite_note-338] [Kerala cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_cuisine) includes dishes like appam, [idiyappam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idiyappam), [puttu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puttu), and [pathiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathiri) and a full-course vegetarian meal is known as *[sadya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadya)*.[^cite_note-339] [Andhra cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_cuisine) is characterized by [pickles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pickling) and spicy [curries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry) with popular dishes including [ulava charu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulava_charu), [bobbatlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobbatlu), [pootharekulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pootharekulu), and [gongura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gongura).[^cite_note-340] [Chettinad cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chettinad_cuisine) is famous for its non-vegetarian items, and [Hyderabadi cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabadi_cuisine) is popular for its [biryani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabadi_biryani).[^cite_note-341] [Udupi cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udupi_cuisine), which originates from [Udupi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udupi) located in the Canara region of Karnataka is famous for its vegetarian dishes and [neer dosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neer_dosa), [ragi mudde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ragi_mudde), [Maddur vada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maddur_vada), [obbattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obbattu), [bisi bele bath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisi_bele_bath), and [Dharwad pedha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharwad_pedha) are some of the popular cuisines of Karnataka.[^cite_note-342] Fish and [sea food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_food) form an integral component of the diet along the coast.[^cite_note-343]

### Music and dance

South Indian dance forms



[Bharatanatyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharatanatyam) (Tamil Nadu)



[Kathakali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathakali) (Kerala)



[Mohiniyattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohiniyattam) (Kerala)



[Kuchipudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuchipudi) (Andhra Pradesh)



[Yakshagana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yakshagana) (Karnataka)

The traditional music of South India is known as [Carnatic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_music), which includes rhythmic and structured music by composers such as [Purandara Dasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purandara_Dasa), [Kanaka Dasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanaka_Dasa), [Tyagayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyagayya), [Annamacharya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annamacharya), [Baktha Ramadasu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhadrachala_Ramadasu), [Muthuswami Dikshitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muthuswami_Dikshitar), [Shyama Shastri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shyama_Shastri), [Kshetrayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kshetrayya), [Mysore Vasudevachar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mysore_Vasudevachar), and [Swathi Thirunal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swathi_Thirunal).[^cite_note-344] [Nadaswaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadaswaram), a [reed instrument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reed_instrument) that is often accompanied by the [thavil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thavil), a type of [drum instrument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_instrument) are the major musical instruments used in temples and weddings.[^cite_note-345]

South India is home to several distinct dance forms with major being [Bharatanatyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharata_Natyam), [Kuchipudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuchipudi), [Kathakali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathakali), [Mohiniaattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohiniaattam) and [Yakshagana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yakshagana).[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347] Other regional folk dances include [Andhra Natyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Natyam), [Karakattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karakattam), [Kavadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavadi), [Kerala Natanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Natanam), [Koodiyattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koodiyattam), [Margamkali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margamkali), [Oppana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oppana), [Ottamthullal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottamthullal), [Oyilattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oyilattam), [Puravaiattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poikkaal_Kuthirai_Aattam) and [Theyyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theyyam).[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] The dance, clothing, and sculptures of South India exemplify the beauty of the body and motherhood.[^cite_note-352]

### Literature



Sage [Agastya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agastya) of the [First Tamil Sangam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Sangams)

South India has an independent literary tradition dating back to over 2500 years. The earliest known literature of South India is the poetic [Sangam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_literature), which was written in Tamil and is dated back to the [Sangam period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangam_period) (300 BCE to 300 CE). [Tamil literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_literature) from the period was composed in three successive poetic assemblies known as [Tamil Sangams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Sangams), the earliest of which, according to ancient tradition, were held on a now vanished [continent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumari_Kandam) far to the south of India.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354] Early Tamil literature include the oldest grammatical treatise, *[Tholkappiyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tholkappiyam)*, and the epics *Silappatikaram* and *[Manimekalai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manimekalai)*.[^cite_note-355]

References to [Kannada literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada_literature) appear from the fourth century CE.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] [Telugu literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_literature) adopted a form of [Prakrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prakrit_language) which in course of development became the ancestor of Telugu.[^cite_note-358] Distinct [Malayalam literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_literature) came later in the 13th century.[^cite_note-359]

### Architecture



The large *[gopuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram)* is a hallmark of [Dravidian architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_architecture)

South India has two distinct styles of rock architecture, the *[Dravidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_architecture)* style of Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh and the *[Vesara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vesara)* style of Karnataka, Telangana.[^cite_note-360] The [architecture of Kerala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Kerala) is unique and emerged from the Dravidian architecture.[^cite_note-361]

In Dravidian architecture, the [temples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_temple_architecture) considered of porches or *[mandapas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandapa)* preceding the door leading to the *[garbagriha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbagriha)* (sanctum). Large *[gopurams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram)* (gate-pyramids) located atop the entrances to large quadrangular enclosures that surround the main sanctum and large pillared halls are usual features of these temples. Besides these, a South Indian temple usually has a tank called the *kalyani* or *[pushkarni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pushkarni)*.[^cite_note-362] The gopuram is a monumental tower, usually ornate at the entrance of the temple forms a prominent feature of [koils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koil) and Hindu temples of the Dravidian style.[^cite_note-363] They are topped by the *kalasam*, a bulbous stone [finial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finial) and function as gateways through the walls that surround the temple complex.[^cite_note-364] The gopuram's origins can be traced back to the [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava_art_and_architecture) who built the group of monuments in [Mahabalipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabalipuram) and Kanchipuram.[^cite_note-UNC-365] The [Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_art_and_architecture) later expanded the same, and by the [Pandya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandyan_art_and_architecture) rule in twelfth century, these gateways became a dominant feature of a temple's outer appearance.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] *[Vimanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vimana_\(architectural_feature\))* are similar structures built over the *garbhagriha* or inner sanctum of the temple. In the Dravidian architecture, these are usually smaller than the gopurams with a few exceptions such as the [Brihadisvara Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brihadisvara_Temple) in [Thanjavur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanjavur).[^cite_note-368]

The Vesara style of architecture originated during the reign of [Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badami_Chalukya_architecture) and later adopted by [Western Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Chalukya_architecture), and [Hoysalas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoysala_architecture).[^cite_note-369] The later [Vijayanagara architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_architecture) incorporated the features of both Dravidian and Vesara styles.[^cite_note-370] The temples at [Mahabalipuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_Monuments_at_Mahabalipuram),[^cite_note-UNC-365] [Thanjavur, Gangaikonda Cholapuram and Darasuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Living_Chola_Temples),[^cite_note-371] [Hampi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampi),[^cite_note-372] and [Pattadakal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pattadakal) are declared [UNESCO World Heritage sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_India).[^cite_note-373]

### Visual arts

See also: [Cinema of South India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_South_India)



[AVM studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AVM_Productions) in Chennai, the oldest surviving studio in India

Films done in regional languages are prevalent in [South India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_South_India), with several regional cinemas being recognized: [Kannada cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada_cinema) (Karnataka), [Malayalam cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam_cinema) (Kerala), [Tamil cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_cinema) (Tamil Nadu), and [Telugu cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_cinema) (Andhra Pradesh and Telangana). The first silent film in South India, *[Keechaka Vadham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keechaka_Vadham)*, was made by [R. Nataraja Mudaliar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R._Nataraja_Mudaliar) in 1916.[^cite_note-374] Mudaliar also established Madras's first film studio.[^cite_note-375] The first Tamil talkie, *[Kalidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalidas_\(film\))*, was released on 31 October 1931, barely seven months after India's first talking picture, *[Alam Ara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alam_Ara)*.[^cite_note-376]

[Swamikannu Vincent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swamikannu_Vincent) built the first cinema studio of South India, at Coimbatore, introducing the "tent cinema", which he first established in Madras and which was known as "Edison's Grand Cinemamegaphone".[^cite_note-377] Filmmakers [K. N. T. Sastry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K._N._T._Sastry) and [B. Narsing Rao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B._Narsing_Rao) in Telugu cinema; [K Balachandar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K_Balachandar), [Balu Mahendra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balu_Mahendra), [Bharathiraaja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharathiraaja), and [Mani Ratnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mani_Ratnam) in Tamil cinema; [Adoor Gopalakrishnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adoor_Gopalakrishnan), [Shaji N. Karun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaji_N._Karun), [John Abraham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Abraham_\(director\)), and [G. Aravindan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G._Aravindan) in Malayalam cinema; and [Girish Kasaravalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girish_Kasaravalli), [Girish Karnad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girish_Karnad) and [P. Sheshadri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._Sheshadri) in Kannada cinema produced realistic cinema in parallel with each other throughout the 1970s.[^cite_note-378]

South Indian cinema has also [had an influence on politics of Tamil Nadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_cinema_and_Dravidian_politics).[^cite_note-379] Prominent film personalities such as [C N Annadurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C_N_Annadurai), [M G Ramachandran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M_G_Ramachandran), [M Karunanidhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M_Karunanidhi), [N. T. Rama Rao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N._T._Rama_Rao), and [Jayalalithaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayalalithaa) have become chief ministers of South Indian states.[^cite_note-380] As of 2014, South Indian film industries contribute to 53% of the total films produced in India.[^cite_note-film-381]

## Utility services

### Health



Chennai, India's 'healthcare capital'

All South Indian states rank in the [top ten in institutional delivery in India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_states_ranking_by_institutional_delivery)[^cite_note-383] and [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_states_by_life_expectancy_at_birth).[^cite_note-384] The region also has a lower [infant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) and [child mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_mortality) compared to the national average.[^cite_note-IMR-140] Chennai attracts about 45 percent of [health tourists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_tourism) coming to India and is known as *India's health capital*.[^cite_note-385] As of 2017, there are 9,482 public health facilities run by the respective state governments. Apart from these, there are 676 [AYUSH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AYUSH) facilities, 11 defence hospitals, 32 hospitals run by Indian Railways, and 39 hospitals run by state insurance corporations.[^cite_note-Health-386]

## Education

As per the 2011 census, the average [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy_rate) in South India is approximately 80%, considerably higher than the Indian national average of 74%, with Kerala having the highest literacy rate of 93.91%.[^cite_note-lit-151]

Major Educational Institutions



[University of Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Madras), one of the oldest and premier university



[IIM Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IIM_Bangalore), one of the top ranked management institutes



[IIT Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IIT_Madras), one of the oldest and prominent IITs



[IISc Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Institute_of_Science), one of the premium research institutes

## Sports



[M. A. Chidambaram Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._A._Chidambaram_Stadium) in Chennai, one of the oldest cricketing venues

Cricket is the most popular sport in South India.[^cite_note-388] The region has five current international cricket venues: [M. A. Chidambaram Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._A._Chidambaram_Stadium) in Chennai, [M. Chinnaswamy Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._Chinnaswamy_Stadium) in Bangalore, [Dr. Y. S. Rajasekhara Reddy International Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._Y._S._Rajasekhara_Reddy_International_Cricket_Stadium) in Vishakapatnam, [Rajiv Gandhi International Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajiv_Gandhi_International_Cricket_Stadium) in Hyderabad and [Greenfield International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenfield_International_Stadium) in Thiruvananthapuram. Six more defunct venues have also hosted international matches in the past.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] [Tennis ball cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_ball_cricket) is played throughout the region.[^cite_note-391] [Indian Premier League](13260340.md) (IPL) is the premium [T20 cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T20_cricket) competition which has three teams from the region namely [Chennai Super Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennai_Super_Kings), [Royal Challengers Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Challengers_Bangalore) and [Sunrisers Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunrisers_Hyderabad). Chennai Super Kings is the most successful franchise in IPL.[^cite_note-392]



[Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi) is the state game of all the states in the region

[Football](10568.md) is also popular with the [Indian Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Super_League) being the major club competition. There are three teams from the region:[Bengaluru FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengaluru_FC), [Chennaiyin FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chennaiyin_FC), and [Kerala Blasters FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerala_Blasters_FC).[^cite_note-393] The Southern Derby or Southern Rivalry, is the name given to a [derby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_derby) contested by any two of the three professional [football clubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_clubs) Bengaluru, Chennaiyin and Kerala Blasters.[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396] [Santosh Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santosh_Trophy) is a football competition amongst states organized by the [Indian Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Football_Association).[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] As of 2022, South Indian teams have won 17 championships.[^cite_note-399]

[Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi) is a [contact sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contact_sport) which is the state game of all the states in South India, and played across the region.[^cite_note-400] [Pro Kabaddi League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Kabaddi_League) is the most popular region based franchise tournament and has three teams representing the region–[Bengaluru Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengaluru_Bulls), [Tamil Thalaivas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Thalaivas) and [Telugu Titans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_Titans).[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402]

[Chess](5134.md) is a popular [board game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Board_game) which originated as [Sathurangam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaturanga) in the seventh century CE.[^cite_note-403] [Traditional games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_games_of_India) like [Pallanguzhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallanguzhi),[^cite_note-404] [Uriyadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Tamil_Nadu#Uriyadi),[^cite_note-405] [Gillidanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gillidanda),[^cite_note-406] [Dhaayam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pachisi)[^cite_note-407] are played across the region. [Jallikattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jallikattu),[^cite_note-408] [Rekla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rekla)[^cite_note-409] and [Kambala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kambala)[^cite_note-tribes-410] are traditional sporting events involving [bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bull). Traditional martial arts include [Silambattam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silambattam),[^cite_note-411] [Gatta gusthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatta_gusthi),[^cite_note-412] [Adimurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adimurai)[^cite_note-413] and [Kalari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalaripayattu).[^cite_note-414] [Vallam kali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallam_kali) is a [boat race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boat_race) organized in Kerala during the monsoon season.[^cite_note-415]
